# P1 implementation status

## Automatic formatter execution evidence (2026-10-10)

Successful write, edit, apply_patch and notebook_edit mutations now run matching enabled formatters sequentially before owned LSP feedback. Discovery uses the same twelve built-in definitions, marker rules, trust-filtered settings and PATH/cache selection as status. Commands replace $FILE with the canonical absolute edited path, use the selected Location, overlay configured environment and retain the selected sandbox and proxy. Automatic commands use owned Session temporary roots rather than the ambient macOS temporary tree; reserved temporary/proxy variables cannot override enforcement, and unknown network domains receive no automatic approval. Disabled/missing/configuration failures are safe, logged outcomes rather than failures of the completed edit.

Configuration/provenance and edited contents are re-observed immediately before launch. Nonzero exits and execution errors are logged without exposing arbitrary stdout/stderr in the tool result; streams are drained with fixed buffers. Every formatter has a thirty-second deadline. Cancellation or timeout terminates its owned process tree and retains the process/sandbox until settlement observation; cancellation starts no later formatter, while ordinary timeout/nonzero failure permits the next formatter. Canonical regular-file rereads refuse redirected targets; Unix no-follow/nonblocking reads also compare file identity after reading. Changed contents and the diff from the submitted edit are JSON-escaped in the result. Existing edit authorization and runtime Location ownership remain authoritative. File locks cover formatting and are released before language-server diagnostics wait.

Seven actual macOS process cases cover absolute arguments, Location cwd, configured environment, ordering/extension selection, nonzero failure with subsequent formatting, sandbox escape refusal, all four edit tools, missing/disabled commands, the actual thirty-second deadline and cancellation with descendant stop. One additional framed-server host case proves didOpen and didSave both receive the formatted text and compiler errors still reach the result. Local validation passes 241 cases: 151 tool library, seventeen edit/LSP feedback, nineteen flow, seven formatter process, ten golden, twelve notebook and thirty-five ordinary tools. After portable descendant-state assertions were adjusted to recognize terminated Unix zombies, all seven formatter process cases pass again. Workspace all-target Clippy with warnings/complexity denied, formatting/diff and generated SDK consistency checks pass. All 59 strict OpenSpec items pass; cross-spec lint reports zero errors and 21 warnings.

Fresh native acceptance remains required. CI at preceding e353556 (run 38051268656) reports macOS failure in all_four_edit_tools_append_fresh_errors_and_write_limits_new_other_files: the edited-file diagnostics are present but zero of five expected other-file blocks were collected. Its native test log is evidence of an unresolved collection/scheduling issue requiring investigation; no timing-only relaxation or unrelated-failure classification is asserted. At observation Windows lint and Ubuntu tests remain live, with SDK, specifications and all six platform builds successful. Native Windows formatter execution, complete diagnostic lifecycle/client adoption and all other P1 contracts remain open. No full milestone is accepted; P0 services/data/artifacts and its independent local-model exit gate are preserved.


## Read-only LSP navigation evidence (2026-10-10)

The built-in lsp tool now supports all eight canonical operations: definition, references, hover, document_symbols, workspace_symbols, implementation, rename_preview and cached diagnostics. It checks lsp permission, remains offered to both model catalogs and plan mode as read-only, and preserves the default ask policy. Unapproved unattended/default/plan/dont-ask calls, explicit deny, disabled settings and invalid admissions start no server. Positive 1-based positions convert to protocol positions; rename preview requires a new name.

Queries share owned Location pools, sticky failures, authority checks, native resources and explicit lifecycle fences with read warming and edit feedback. Workspace queries can discover/start configured installed roots without opening a synthetic document and retain existing nested roots. Activity covers discovery, RPC and output collection. File queries capture managed ancestry and bounded canonical UTF-8 contents, revalidate before startup, and combine document open/RPC into one serialized worker operation after a retained current-content observation. A queued changed snapshot is refused before didOpen; caller source changes during RPC discard results without altering user content. Existing scope claims remain retained through native/resource settlement.

Output projects locations/links, flat/hierarchical symbols and hover content into at most fifty Location-relative rows across servers, with 1-based typed positions, path:line:col strings, bounded line previews and omitted/failed counts. Remote/external URIs, malformed/reversed/oversized ranges and invalid link selections are withheld. Source previews use the shared bounded regular-file observation refusing symlinks/reparse points. Unicode and literal backslashes are preserved. Rename preview prefers ordered documentChanges over changes, includes proposed text and file/directory operations and retains supplied versions plus server/root provenance. It writes no source/destination and sends no didSave. Cached diagnostics retain optional server versus observed-client version metadata and revalidate changed files.

A queue ownership regression additionally exposes a deadlock in main's original enqueue routine: a closed receiver rejects an owned save while holding table/activity locks, then activity disposal reacquires those locks. An isolated reproduction enters the single regression test and remains live until its external watchdog terminates that verification group. With the fix restored the same test completes, and closed/full queue cases both release activity. Rejected job disposal now occurs after releasing queue activity locks.

Local real-server host cases exercise all eight operations, sixty references capped at fifty, external/remote omission, coordinates/previews, terminal-control escaping, plan-mode rename non-mutation including directories, version/provenance, cached diagnostic revalidation, cold workspace discovery, model catalogs, denial/default ask, disabled/malformed admissions, safe remote failure, mid-RPC user changes and literal filename handling. Portable output validation and the complete per-tool golden include successful LSP navigation; nested-root and queued-content subprocess cases pass. The native fixture explicitly disables other installed built-in servers so these cases invoke only its selected command. No complete canonical lsp tool or full milestone acceptance is inferred before fresh platform evidence.

Local validation passes 285 cases across the tool library (150), tool/protocol/runtime integration (121), managed-checkout LSP integration (eight) and App intelligence (six). After final output review, three portable output cases pass, including the added malformed workspace-symbol range and numeric-kind case, and the sixteen real-server edit/navigation cases are rerun. Workspace all-target Clippy with warnings denied, formatting/diff and generated SDK consistency checks pass. All 59 strict OpenSpec items pass; cross-spec lint reports zero errors and 21 warnings. Fresh native platform acceptance remains pending.

Sandboxed formatter execution, optional installers, broader delayed unversioned-diagnostic ambiguity, client-wide/live CLI lifecycle integration, enforced Windows launch and all other P1 contracts remain required. P0 local-model processes/data/artifacts and its independent exit gate are preserved.


## Patch deletion and rename synchronization evidence (2026-10-10)

Applied deletions now queue owned synchronization to matching existing server roots. They use a canonical parent, revalidate path absence and pre-mutation managed ancestry, and freshly check running service authority. Deletions do not start servers solely for missing files. Open documents receive didClose; document/quiet-clock and typed diagnostic cache state is cleared without resetting the generation version counter. Deleted-file watched events follow closure. Successful saves now send created/changed watched events based on captured pre-mutation existence. Rename synchronizes source deletion before destination open/save in a shared server/root. These mutation events do not implement general filesystem watching or dynamic watcher registration.

Removal admission reuses retained native ownership and acquires missing independent claims for every enclosing managed checkout even when the deleted document was never opened. A real macOS unmanaged-root case deletes a file inside two nested managed checkouts, sends only its deletion event, refuses force-removal of both checkouts and keeps ownership through cancelled shutdown until native/resource acknowledgement. A real subprocess case blocks removal admission and proves descendants stop before the blocked claim is released; final/repeated close retains acknowledgement semantics.

Eight real-server host cases cover all four edits plus ordered rename/deletion, created/changed/deleted event types, no stale old-path blocks, owned zero-wait deletion, cold no-start and unmatched no-notification behavior. A portable absence-observation test refuses recreated files, noncanonical parents and dangling Unix symlinks. Local validation passes 55 LSP library, 112 tool/protocol/runtime integration and eight managed-checkout LSP cases (175 total). One initial concurrent managed run timed out at idle-start readiness; the isolated case and complete eight-case rerun both pass. Workspace all-target Clippy with warnings/complexity denied, formatting/diff and generated SDK consistency checks pass. All 59 strict OpenSpec items pass; cross-spec lint reports zero errors and 21 warnings. Fresh native platform CI remains required.

The complete canonical Diagnostics after edits contract remains unaccepted pending broader delayed unversioned-publication handling and native platform evidence. Navigation, sandboxed formatters, client-wide lifecycle controls, enforced Windows launch and all other P1 requirements remain in scope. No full milestone is accepted. P0 local-model processes/data/artifacts and its independent exit gate are preserved.


## Document feedback debounce and supersession evidence (2026-10-10)

Diagnostic collection now uses a shared per-document quiet deadline updated after each successful didSave, including identical-content saves. Waiting callers consult the current server-generation deadline, so a rapid later save defers both collections; each caller retains its original configured timeout. All save notifications remain delivered. Quiet-clock state follows the bounded document cache and is removed on eviction.

Save receipts retain the immutable admitted content/managed-creation observation. Before collecting primary or other-file feedback, workers reject superseded client versions or changed observations and complete the old waiter with no blocks. This also covers user edits without another save notification. It preserves the distinction between optional server versions and observed client state; an unversioned publication is still not proof that the server computed a particular edit.

Three real sandboxed-server regressions fail against main at f87f8c5: an earlier identical-save waiter ignores the later quiet period, and superseded/external changes still leak other-file errors. All three pass with the changes restored. Six host feedback cases, 53 LSP library the remaining 104 tool/protocol/runtime integration cases and seven managed-checkout LSP cases pass locally (170 total). Workspace all-target Clippy with warnings/complexity denied, formatting/diff and generated SDK consistency checks pass. All 59 strict OpenSpec items pass; cross-spec lint reports zero errors and 21 warnings. Prior revision f87f8c5 is genuinely in progress in CI run 38047241389; fresh native acceptance remains required.

Deletion/rename notifications, broader delayed unversioned-publication ambiguity, navigation, sandboxed formatters, client-wide lifecycle controls, enforced Windows launch and the complete remaining P1 scope stay open. No full milestone is accepted, and the canonical Diagnostics after edits contract remains unaccepted pending complete lifecycle/native evidence. P0 local-model processes/data/artifacts and its independent exit gate are preserved.


## Edit-tool diagnostic feedback evidence (2026-10-10)

All four built-in edit tools now capture managed ancestry before writing, release their file-write lock and enqueue owned LSP save synchronization. Matching servers receive didOpen/didChange and didSave after fresh authority, creation and disk-content checks; save text is included when requested by server capabilities. Configured waiting covers queued discovery/save and diagnostic retrieval. A timeout or unavailable service leaves the successful file edit successful. Zero-wait synchronization retains service activity and notification ownership after its caller stops waiting.

Feedback considers publications received after save admission and revalidates current disk contents/document versions. It renders escaped explicit errors, twenty per file with the remaining count; write merges up to five other files and excludes unchanged previously cached errors. The existing output budget prioritizes actual diagnostic tails using a typed flag. Multi-file patches finish their applied mutations before collecting feedback, including applied changes on partial failure.

Three real macOS sandboxed-server host cases cover all four tools, requested save text, versions, completed multi-file patch state, error/warning filtering, escaping/counts, other-file bounds and unchanged-error suppression, diagnostic timeout, owned zero-wait save delivery, concurrent writes during a diagnostic wait and cancellation preserving saved content. The 53 LSP library cases, 107 tool/protocol/runtime integration cases and seven managed-checkout LSP cases pass locally (167 total). Workspace all-target Clippy with warnings and complexity denied, formatting/diff and generated SDK consistency checks pass. All 59 strict OpenSpec items pass; cross-spec lint reports zero errors and 21 warnings. Fresh platform CI remains required.

This is implementation evidence, not acceptance of the complete canonical Diagnostics after edits contract. The current 150 ms delayed collection does not prove coalescing of rapid saves; deletion/rename notifications, broader stale-publication races and native Windows execution remain required. Unversioned publications preserve their omitted version and receipt timing is not proof that the server computed a particular edit. Formatters, navigation, client-wide lifecycle controls and the full remaining P1 scope stay open. No milestone is accepted; P0 services/data/artifacts and its separate exit gate remain preserved.


## M1.4 runtime Location activity leases (2026-10-10)

Language-service idle expiry now uses shared canonical Location activity as well as admitted warming. Activity acquisition creates no pool or process and can precede first warming. Concurrent guards disable the idle deadline; final disposal starts a full fresh idle window and wakes the service coordinator. The counter/deadline and idle cancellation decision share one lock. Explicit close remains available during activity, and reload preserves live guards for the replacement generation. Services and explicit fences retain their 128-Location bound. Activity follows owned operations without consuming service slots or rejecting model admission when that cache is full; disposal without retained services/fences removes its tracking.

Built-in Location admission acquires this activity before owned repository inspection and composes it with the existing managed/unmanaged lease. Worktree creation identity and independent checkout claims are unchanged. Retained native settlement returns a proof owning both checkout locks and activity through the consuming commit. This is connected to runtime Drains and idle operations through the existing ToolHost admission port, and to synthetic hook-test admission through the same host path. It grants no file/process authority and does not reconstruct activity from stored Sessions.

Four registry/native library cases verify lazy acquisition/full-cache independence/tracking disposal, shared canonical activity across close/reload, and a real server staying connected beyond idle until the last guard is disposed. Two real macOS managed-checkout cases verify a runtime shell tool without further warming keeps LSP connected beyond its idle interval, and retained native Location proof does likewise; both settle after final release. The preceding `03edd6d` Windows lint/test, macOS/Ubuntu tests, SDK/specifications and all six platform builds pass in run 38040158871; its Windows build remains live at observation. Fresh native acceptance for this increment remains required.

Validation passes 130 Rust cases: 45 LSP library, 25 managed worktree/setup, 19 runtime flow, 35 built-in tool and six App TCP cases. Both real managed activity cases pass again after the final cache-capacity change. Workspace all-target Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, diff and generated SDK consistency checks pass. All 59 strict OpenSpec items pass; cross-spec lint reports zero errors and 21 warnings. Public HTTP schemas/SDK sources are unchanged by this activity increment.

Client-wide Location activity outside runtime admission, live CLI/TUI lifecycle controls, reviewed unknown recovery, diagnostic document/path/version validation, edit synchronization, navigation/formatting, enforced Windows launch and the broader P1 contracts remain open. No canonical full lifecycle contract or complete milestone is accepted. P0 local-model work/data and its independent acceptance are preserved.

## M1.4 explicit LSP Location transitions (2026-10-10)

Location-scoped close now fences new warming before waiting, retains the same discovery/native cleanup worker across cancelled callers, and keeps the Location closed until explicit reload. Reload joins the observed generation and requires complete native/resource acknowledgement, with a narrow retry for a joined discovery error that created no pool. Unverified discovery joins and unacknowledged roots refuse reopening. Per-transition tokens prevent an older waiting reload from undoing a newer close. Other Locations remain independent; active and explicitly fenced Locations share the 128-entry bound. Reload clears the settled generation and reopens lazy admission without discovery or startup.

Authenticated body-free POST /api/v1/lsp/close and /api/v1/lsp/reload now delegate through App services and the built-in host. Responses retain the selected Location and report prior generation closure/reopened admission. Unsettled or superseded transitions return conflict; custom hosts return typed LSP ServiceUnavailableError. OpenAPI and the generated SDK expose lsp.close() and lsp.reload(). README capabilities describe the scoped controls.

Validation passes 107 Rust cases (41 LSP library, six App TCP, 60 server HTTP), SDK type checking and all 80 SDK tests. The OpenAPI snapshot and generated-source check pass. Workspace all-target Clippy with warnings denied, formatting and diff checks pass. All 59 strict OpenSpec items pass; cross-spec lint reports zero errors and 21 warnings. Fresh native platform CI remains required for this increment.

Portable registry cases cover empty closed Locations, lazy reload, discovery retention after cancelled close, reload superseded by a newer close, known discovery retry, unverified discovery refusal and unacknowledged root refusal. A real Unix hung-shutdown case proves cancelled scoped close retains native cleanup while a sibling stays connected, then acknowledged reload permits a new generation. App TCP tests cover authentication, invalid bodies/Locations, query-over-header routing and no startup; custom-host HTTP tests cover unavailable lifecycle routes. Complete Location activity/lease integration, live CLI/TUI controls, reviewed unknown recovery, diagnostics/edit/navigation/formatters, enforced Windows launch and the broader P1 contracts remain open. No milestone is accepted; P0 local-model data and acceptance are preserved.

## M1.4 document checkout admission (2026-10-10)

Read warming now captures document checkout ancestry as well as Location ancestry before reading bytes. The immutable document scope travels through delayed discovery, startup and queued didOpen/didChange. Canonical file identity and the complete managed creation set are rechecked on joined blocking work before delivery. A running LocalLauncher service acquires any missing enclosing checkout claims through the existing owned sandboxed Git port, using independent `lsp` owners. Both original and later document leases remain retained through native/proxy acknowledgement and worker disposal; document eviction does not release them. The service refuses changed creations at previously claimed paths and caps its total admitted checkout scopes at 128. Low-level pools without a claim implementation refuse managed documents.

A real macOS server rooted outside the managed checkouts now opens a document inside two nested managed checkouts. It receives one didOpen for duplicate snapshots; opening 128 additional documents evicts that snapshot with a real didClose while both leases remain held. Changed creation at that claimed path is refused. Actual removal of both checkouts is blocked, remains blocked through cancelled close, and succeeds after native acknowledgement/disposal. A portable delayed-discovery case deletes/recreates only the document checkout while the outer Location remains unchanged and proves no stale launch. A Unix blocked-claim case proves native descendants stop before the retained claim is released, with cancelled/repeated close and no document delivery after cancellation. The existing startup pin and replaced-Location cases still pass.

The preceding `bb136b8` CI run 38038098529 has successful Ubuntu tests, SDK/specification checks and all six Linux/macOS platform builds. Its macOS tests and Windows lint/test job remain live at observation; fresh native acceptance for this increment is required.

## M1.4 Location-owned LSP warming and live status (2026-10-10)

App assembly now attaches a canonical Location registry using fresh trusted local launch, shared sandbox policy/helper and catalog credential names. Successful authorized UTF-8 text reads enqueue background snapshots without waiting for discovery, initialization or diagnostics. Discovery is owned blocking work; one immutable pool generation is shared per Location. Root workers send one didOpen for unchanged snapshots and versioned full-content didChange for changed reads. They retain at most 128 document digests/versions and send didClose on eviction. Snapshots above 1 MiB are skipped, warm queues are capped at 16 per Location, and at most 128 Locations are admitted. These bounds do not accept the complete read-warming/diagnostics contract.

Host shutdown fences new warming and joins retained service workers alongside MCP shutdown. Cancelled waits preserve the same join handles and cached results. Generations expire after 60 minutes without admitted warming and are recreated only after acknowledged native/resource settlement; full Location activity/lease integration and explicit reload/close controls remain required. Authenticated GET /api/v1/lsp and generated SDK `lsp.status()` observe actual starting/connected/broken roots without discovery/startup, respecting Location routing and excluding configured commands/environment.

Local evidence includes 33 LSP library cases, including real didOpen/didChange/didClose traffic, idle settlement/recreation and cancelled Location close during an actual hung native shutdown. All 35 built-in tool cases pass, including a text read returning while discovery is deliberately blocked and host close retaining that discovery. Five actual App TCP cases pass, including authenticated LSP observation, a real failed root, query/header routing and secret exclusion. Custom-host unavailable status and OpenAPI snapshot checks pass; SDK type checking and all 78 cases pass. The preceding launch/resource revision's Windows LSP protocol and resource steps passed in [run 38033188778](https://github.com/CyberdyneCorp/cybercode-cli/actions/runs/38033188778/job/114158178414) while its full job remained live. ## M1.4 continuous owned LSP message handling (2026-10-10)

The client now retains a dedicated framing reader and a bounded four-frame inbox. Idle worker selection cancels only an inbox wait, so partially read stdout headers/bodies survive health checks and foreground commands. The worker consumes idle notifications and answers supported/refused server requests without a foreground RPC. Unsolicited responses and malformed idle messages fence reuse and break the root. Notifications remain bounded untrusted data; no server-requested edit is applied. Closing forces overdue native processes before joining the reader, preserving the existing three-second kill deadline. Explicit cleanup aborts/joins reader ownership, while Drop supplies no completion acknowledgement.

Six platform-neutral idle cases pass: partial header/body cancellation followed by a usable RPC, idle request refusal, malformed/foreign envelopes, truncation, stream release and bounded-inbox backpressure with blocked-reader release. Three new Unix subprocess cases verify idle diagnostics/refusal with unchanged file content, broken-root native settlement and a foreground command arriving during actual partial stdout frames. Twelve connection and eleven pool subprocess cases pass, alongside all existing framing/RPC/admission regressions. The preceding native framing/RPC/pool-admission step at `bf17269` passed in [run 38030821975](https://github.com/CyberdyneCorp/cybercode-cli/actions/runs/38030821975/job/114151215610) while its full job remained live; fresh native idle-reader and Windows owned-process acceptance remain required.

This completes continuous raw-message consumption in the shared worker, not document/path/version validation or public diagnostic feedback. Concrete trusted sandbox launch, Location service lifecycle/status, read warming, document synchronization, validated diagnostics/navigation and sandboxed formatter execution remain required. The full P1 scope stays active; no milestone is accepted and P0 local-model acceptance remains independent.

## M1.4 owned LSP pool (2026-10-10)

The Location pool now freezes detected definitions and starts one background worker per server and confined nearest-marker root. Concurrent starts deduplicate; commands use a bounded serialized queue, and failed or unexpectedly exited roots remain broken until pool recreation. Status comes from actual worker state. Explicit close fences admission, cancels workers and joins retained task handles; cancelling close leaves settlement restartable. Sandbox/proxy keepalives survive native settlement, and an unacknowledged startup error retains the actual connection for retry. Last-owner Drop requests cleanup without claiming acknowledgement.

Eight local Unix subprocess pool cases cover concurrent actual processes/requests, independent roots, sticky failures, cancellation during initialization, cancelled-close restart, keepalive release, idle leader death, Drop with a retained client handle and an independently cancelled caller after witnessed request admission. The retained-owner retry case exercises a real process with a seeded unavailable-acknowledgement witness; it is not evidence of an injected native OS acknowledgement failure. Platform-neutral admission tests cover refused lazy startup, disabled/unavailable/missing/unmatched/external files and no extra launch. Existing protocol/framing cases remain required regressions. ## M1.4 owned LSP connection (2026-10-10)

The tools crate now consumes an already-authorized process through an owned LSP connection. It uses a 45-second default initialization deadline, explicit initialized notification, retained capabilities and immutable workspace root. Single-request ownership fences timeouts and cancellation; remote rejection uses safe code-only diagnostics. Server requests do not authorize edits, and untrusted notifications and stderr have bounded retention. Closing attempts shutdown/exit for three seconds before forced tree termination and separate native acknowledgement. Ownership is established synchronously, so even an unpolled startup future terminates its tree when dropped. A retained connection can retry unavailable settlement; Drop itself supplies no acknowledgement.

Eleven local Unix subprocess cases pass, covering initialization/requests, rejection, stalled startup/shutdown, timeout/cancellation, bounded stderr, unpolled startup and descendant cleanup on graceful close and Drop. Four platform-neutral RPC cases and nine framing regressions pass. The dedicated Windows transport step now includes both RPC and framing targets; native execution of the new RPC cases and native owned-process acceptance remain required. The preceding framing-only step at `e3c36c1` has passed in [run 38029132605](https://github.com/CyberdyneCorp/cybercode-cli/actions/runs/38029132605/job/114146186610), whose full job remains live at observation. This is not yet a lazy service pool or public integration: sandbox launch/admission, deduplicated root-scoped ownership, sticky broken state, read warming, document diagnostics/navigation, running status and formatter execution remain required. All P1 milestones/contracts and the independent P0 local-model gate remain in scope.

## M1.4 LSP transport framing (2026-10-10)

The shared tools crate now has bounded Content-Length framing for the owned LSP lifecycle to consume. Headers are limited to 8 KiB and bodies to 4 MiB; UTF-8 byte counts preserve adjacent and fragmented message boundaries. Invalid or duplicate lengths, unsupported encodings, malformed JSON and truncated frames refuse without echoing server content. Interrupted reads/writes fence both directions rather than replaying or interpreting partial messages. Nine local transport cases and workspace all-target Clippy pass. Strict OpenSpec validates 59 items; cross-spec lint reports zero errors and the existing 21 warnings. A dedicated early Windows CI step executes the same transport cases; fresh native execution is pending.

## M1.4 directory-bound memory reads and scope ownership (2026-10-09)

Core memory storage now admits project/global directories through retained capability directory handles and no-follow opens. Explicit create admission uses private Unix directories; existing review creates no directories or note/index files. Scope claims create a private lock file and report contention without blocking, including across processes. Actual process-death tests verify OS lock release without adopting stored execution as live. Symlinked scope/note/index/lock paths and Unix hard-linked files are refused; private-file checks and filename/frontmatter identity checks precede content return. Invalid note records report safe diagnostics, while valid metadata is sorted.

Index reads consume at most 25,004 bytes, producing the first 200 lines or 25,000 UTF-8 bytes with the canonical truncation notice; they never open individual note bodies. Note reads are bounded to 1 MiB; catalog metadata is bounded to 4 MiB and enumeration to 4096 directory entries, with explicit refusal rather than partial success. A retained .memory-transaction entry fences read/list/index and is left intact. This is a read-side ownership/fencing foundation: no transaction creation, note/index writes or automatic reconciliation is implemented yet. Unix permissions are enforced locally; Windows ACL privacy and complete native acceptance remain open.

Local validation passes 94 Rust cases across core units, configuration, paths, memory documents and actual storage tests; workspace all-target Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, generated SDK consistency and 59 strict OpenSpec items pass. Cross-spec lint reports zero errors and 21 warnings. Windows CI now includes the portable memory/document/storage suites, including actual process-death lock release. Journaled mutation/recovery, memory tool permissions/toggles, Context Source reconciliation and HTTP/SDK/CLI/TUI integration remain required; all P1 milestones remain unaccepted.

## M1.4 memory document foundation (2026-10-09)

The authorized `implement-m1-4-memory-intelligence` change covers the complete memory, code-intelligence and browser-verification milestone. The first core increment adds validated Markdown/YAML documents, kebab-case names, known memory types, one-line descriptions and mandatory nonempty Why/How to apply lines for feedback/project notes. Write admission scans frontmatter and body for credential assignments, known key prefixes, private keys and high-entropy strings longer than 32 Unicode characters; diagnostics omit supplied text. Serialization revalidates directly constructed documents. Deterministic index construction sorts unique names, escapes descriptions and refuses duplicate entries. Index snapshots use the first 200 lines or 25,000 UTF-8 bytes with the canonical truncation notice.

Typed enabled/generate settings default true and honor EnvSource's CYBER_DISABLE_MEMORY flag. Actual configuration loading now rejects invalid memory object/boolean types. Path derivation uses the configured data directory and safe project/global identity components. These functions grant no filesystem authority, create no memory files and expose no memory tool or client route. The existing Paths initializer still creates the empty memory root. Private/recoverable storage, cross-process ownership, tool permissions and toggles, Session context/reconciliation, HTTP/SDK/CLI/TUI, LSP/formatters and browser verification remain required. ## Synchronous local MCP-tool hooks (2026-10-09)

Recorded tool/permission hook dispatch and synthetic CLI testing now support configured local `mcp_tool` handlers. Each receipt owns a separate native connection; it never borrows the shared RPC connection or a Session's callback authority. Fresh hook and server authorization precede launch and calls and withhold changed-definition results. Filtered discovery and schema validation precede tool effects. Event JSON and typed argument templates, strict unambiguous textual decisions, default-private receipts and per-Session/per-test once claims use existing hook contracts.

Required hook scope confinement overrides ordinary MCP sandbox opt-out and full-access settings. One absolute handler deadline covers initialization, discovery and calls. Startup cancellation joins initialization/listing ownership; call cancellation or timeout closes the native tree and local proxy before durable receipt settlement and checkout/scratch cleanup. Unacknowledged owners retain unknown evidence. Actual tests prove built-in write denial, separate native connections, typed synthetic input and once behavior without Session admission, schema/filter refusal, malformed/error replies under both fail-closed settings, cancellation and live server/hook-definition changes. A reentrant sampling permission callback successfully calls the same configured server through its dedicated hook connection without a deadlock or human prompt. The real CLI exercises MCP effects and receipt creation without Session startup.

Local validation passes 197 Rust cases across twelve hook/MCP and CLI integration suites, workspace all-target Clippy with warnings denied, all 62 TypeScript SDK tests/typecheck, generated SDK consistency, formatting and all 58 strict OpenSpec items. Cross-spec lint reports zero errors and 21 warnings. Remote MCP hooks, async scheduling, remaining lifecycle delivery, plugins and full native platform acceptance remain open. This is local implementation evidence; the canonical MCP-hook requirement and all P1 milestones remain unaccepted.

## M1.3 bounded fixed-input command concurrency

PermissionRequest and post-tool command handlers now execute through a per-event pool bounded by `hooks.concurrency`. Selection uses their fixed final input; per-event command deduplication remains synchronized. Out-of-order completion frees worker slots immediately, while results merge in declared order. Mandatory stop and fail-closed admission errors cancel event siblings and drain launched owners before returning the original refusal. Queued handlers check cancellation before admission and launch. PreToolUse remains sequential to preserve rewrite-dependent inputs and selectors; its full concurrency model remains required.

Four real-process dispatch regressions prove simultaneous start at a limit of two, no third launch until a slot is free, immediate refill while an earlier handler remains running, duplicate execution once, reverse completion with declared-order denial, owned interruption with no queued effect, and fail-closed refusal that retains its reason while siblings acknowledge termination. This increment does not implement async hooks, other handler transports or all lifecycle owners.

The dedicated [Windows lint job at `9094310`](https://github.com/CyberdyneCorp/cybercode-cli/actions/runs/37846150533/job/113547414708) now completes and exposes its exact failure: the scratch DirBuilder is unnecessarily mutable on Windows because only the Unix permission branch mutates it. The binding is now mutable only in that branch; Unix retains 0700. A portable scratch test and native CI step check unique paths, ordinary cleanup and retention for unknown owners. Native lint acceptance of the correction is still required; this does not establish the causes of earlier unavailable-log failures.

## M1.3 command permission-request dispatch

Built-in approval now emits PermissionRequest immediately before publishing a pending user ask. Recorded command handlers receive captured invocation identity, final tool input and the current ask's action, resources, patterns and metadata. Allow answers only that ask without saving a permission rule; deny blocks without a user prompt or tool effect. Event parsing ignores input rewrites. Ordinary hard deny rules and Mode ceilings are evaluated before this boundary, and already allowed calls do not run request handlers.

Actual dispatch tests cover unattended one-shot approval, Location-relative path selection, ask payload and receipt privacy, rejected input rewriting, no saved rule, denial of an explicit pre-hook ask in bypass Mode, ordinary deny/plan refusal and bypass calls that require no ask. An explicit pre-hook ask now checks current/ancestor auto blocks before entering the request boundary: a regression first reproduces an unauthorized write through request allow, then passes after correction. The request-dispatch future is boxed to bound nested setup stack usage; the previously overflowing App setup-recovery test passes after that correction. This remains command/built-in integration: PermissionDenied retry, other lifecycle owners, remote tools and other handler types remain open. Full native acceptance and complete M1.3 delivery are still required.

## M1.3 durable command once claims

Command `once: true` now pins an effective-handler digest claim at atomic durable receipt admission. A partial unique index and writer-side check admit at most one attempt per Session across runtimes. Restart, completion, cancellation, pre-launch failure and unknown outcomes retain the claim; changed definitions have a new digest and still require current trust. Unrecorded launches refuse once handlers before scratch creation or effects. Existing receipts without the new flag remain readable as ordinary executions.

Recorded command status/completion messages now use transient user notices after admission and acknowledged settlement. Duplicate once matches publish neither message again, and neither message enters model context. Runtime tests cover two-runtime races, unknown restart, distinct Sessions, immutable settlement claims and changed digests; actual tool dispatch covers two calls, one process effect and message privacy. Concurrency, async admission and once scheduling for the other handler types remain required; native acceptance is pending and the complete M1.3 contract remains open.

Strict path resolution and file-link checks now cover ordinary filesystem operands as well as every implicit copy destination. Multiply linked files retain approval because a path-only check cannot establish the other names of their content. These checks apply before automatic Mode allowance and preserve explicit denial. The actual single-file hard-link regression fails on unchanged production main `701a91e`, where the tool returns successful execution instead of refusing unattended approval; the updated code refuses before effects. Tests also cover implicit destination hard links and dangling file/directory aliases, preserving protected data and ordinary file-operation allowance. The literal path fixture uses a real temporary root on every platform instead of assuming `/repo` exists.

## Literal directory copies and moves in accept-edits

Literal `cp -r|-R|--recursive` and directory `mv` now inspect up to 4096 source objects across a command's directory operands and include every source descendant and mapped destination in the permission facts. Existing destination directories receive the source basename; fresh destinations receive the source tree directly. Destination symlink aliases resolve before Location and protected-path checks. Source symlinks, dangling destination aliases, multiply linked source/destination files, unreadable/nonordinary entries, excessive inventories, ambiguous trailing-slash/dot operands and unsupported options retain ordinary approval. Explicit deny rules still refuse execution. This extends the command path analysis; it does not replace OS enforcement or prove remaining option/context semantics.

The actual directory-copy/move regression fails on unchanged production main `fcebb4f` before implementation. Local tests cover copy/move effects, source retention, explicit denial, protected descendants, protected/outside destination aliases, source links, hard links and the shared inventory limit. A native Windows path-proof step is added; native behavior remains unaccepted. Windows file-link checks use an owned metadata handle and `GetFileInformationByHandle`, since Rust's [Windows `number_of_links` metadata API](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/os/windows/fs/trait.MetadataExt.html#tymethod.number_of_links) remains unstable. Complete M1.1 and the full P1 goal remain open; P0 evidence is unchanged.

## Confirmed one-shot replay

`/approve` selects the most recent classifier-backed block, shows its stored tool/input and reason, and replays it once after explicit confirmation. POST `/api/v1/sessions/:id/approve` and SDK `client.session.approve(id)` return a receipt before confirmation. An active coding Drain can continue; confirmed replay waits for it to settle without starting another coding-model turn. The TUI keeps controls visible while scrolling long call details and offers no permanent approval.

A writer-validated record binds decision identity, fresh replay identity, checkout, owned native activity and original admission authority. Pending claims exclude other replays; confirmed consumption is durable before dispatch and remains spent after failure or restart. Reject, unattended resolution and pre-consumption cancellation have no effects and permit a new confirmation. The private grant matches the original Session, call, tool and exact input, expires at invocation completion, and cannot become an ordinary call after expiry. Current hard/config block rules, plan mode, protected paths and independent parent manual approval retain precedence. Stored edit-tool choice is preserved, including apply_patch; unavailable, rewound or unknown calls refuse execution. Normal dispatch/settlement retains uncertain native outcomes. Tool settlement commits independently of user-history admission: a closed scope can retain a known cancellation acknowledgement while refusing new input. The stop regression failed before this separation in the new replay implementation and now passes.

Final local validation passes 1057 workspace Rust executions (three ignored entries, 112 suites), 53 SDK tests, workspace all-target Clippy with warnings denied, formatting, generated-contract consistency and all 57 strict specifications. Cross-spec lint reports zero errors and 21 warnings. Tests include real write/patch effects, repeated/exclusive approval refusal, cancellation/stale-reply refusal, policy-block exclusion, changed denies/plan/parent mode, active-Drain waiting, scoped grant identity/lifetime, acknowledged subtree stop after admission closure and consumed replay refusal in a second runtime. Native acceptance remains pending; reviewed orphan/unknown recovery, complete ancestor classifier/config semantics, remaining interpreter proofs and Windows confinement still prevent full M1.1 acceptance. No P1 milestone is accepted; P0 model/measurement artifacts are unchanged.

The shared foreground command process interface now accepts an invocation-owned AppContainer process. Ordinary Tokio child pipes and native asynchronous pipes expose the same read interface to the shell output pump. The Windows adapter closes unused stdin immediately, preserves native exit codes and retains original process ownership during borrowed waits. Cancelling a borrowed wait leaves the owner available for explicit termination and acknowledgement; disposing the enclosing owner still terminates the native tree. Owned waits retain their disposal behavior.

A Windows-only `AppContainerProcess` export adapts a supplied native process; it is not a new HTTP/CLI execution route. ## Owned AppContainer command streams

The native launcher now has a `spawn_piped` owner with asynchronous host stdin/stdout/stderr, backed by unique local single-instance directional byte pipes. It connects each synchronous client endpoint and verifies its host process identity before launching. The existing suspended-launch, exact-token verification and three-handle inheritance path remains the launch boundary. Temporary host client copies close immediately after creation so they cannot prolong output EOF. Consumers can take each host stream once and drain stdout/stderr while waiting. Host endpoints use [Tokio’s directional named-pipe options](https://docs.rs/tokio/1.53.2/tokio/net/windows/named_pipe/struct.ServerOptions.html).

Explicit termination closes the owned job while retaining the original process handle for acknowledgement. Dropping the owner or its wait future still terminates the process tree; unused stdin closes before waiting. New native cases cover Unicode and 128 KiB input with separate 512 KiB outputs, EOF, failed-launch retry, owner disposal, unpolled/aborted waits, pending read completion and explicit termination. CI runs these cases independently as well as in the complete native launch suite.

Local validation passes all 15 sandbox tests, sandbox all-target Clippy, formatting and all 57 strict specifications; cross-spec lint reports zero errors and 21 warnings. ## Ancestor auto classifier intersection

Eligible child actions now pass every effective auto-mode gate before effects. Each ancestor uses its own trusted configuration policy, recent messages, stated boundaries and Location; evaluator evidence also identifies the actual child execution Location. An automatic allow at one gate cannot replace another gate's classifier review. Explicit block rules across all gates precede inference and confirmed overrides, and a deny fallback anywhere in the intersection refuses execution. Independent manual Modes, protected paths and irreversible-action ceilings retain precedence.

The runtime validates ancestor identity and effective Mode before reviewing, serializes the entire batch, and updates consecutive blocks once per action. Intermediate allows cannot reset a later ancestor block; after three blocked actions the next classifier request falls back without inference. Every decision and evaluator usage remain durable on the child, with optional reviewed-ancestor identity for backward-compatible replay and existing descendant billing. Confirmed one-shot replay also handles an ancestor classifier block without saving approval or invoking another coding turn.

The production admission regression fails on unchanged main `9b196c3`: the ancestor's eligible allow never reaches classification, and the child edit remains refused. This is evidence of missing review/allow behavior, not an unauthorized prior edit. Focused validation passes all 37 production auto-dispatch cases and runtime evaluator cases, including distinct parent/child Locations, grandparent rejection after parent allowance, child-billed replay, three-block fallback, manual ceiling, deny fallback, confirmed replay and invalid review ancestry. The full workspace passes 1064 Rust test executions (three ignored entries, 112 suites); all 57 strict specifications pass and cross-spec lint reports zero errors and 21 warnings. Workspace all-target Clippy with warnings denied and formatting pass. After extracting per-gate evaluation to satisfy the complexity limit, all 37 production and 12 runtime evaluator cases pass again. Native CI includes both suites. Full M1.1 acceptance, org-policy integration, remaining interpreter proofs and Windows confinement remain open.

## Matched verified subtree reopening

The runtime, authenticated POST `/api/v1/sessions/{sessionID}/reopen-subtree` and generated `session.reopenSubtree` now reopen only the reviewed current scope and latest persisted Acknowledged stop receipt. Stop reports expose their receipt and unique sweep identity. Every sweep persists start/terminal ownership; another pending or orphaned sweep cannot be hidden by an earlier acknowledgement.

Reopening replays fresh durable Session state, verifies projection identity and contiguous history, and refuses unsettled Steps/tools, preparation, Jobs and private ownership ledgers. Verified native Location identity and acknowledged locks remain retained through writer commit. The projector rebuilds the subtree evidence and compares Session creation/latest event identities, revisions, scoped Job hashes and operation event identities atomically. Changed evidence returns a conflict. Callbacks targeting unrelated Sessions retain their original authority, and stop/reopening audit the initial operation reservation even after terminal records clear their bindings. Two capture/writer regressions fail on unchanged main `93cd10e`; a third verifies unknown idle provenance. Only the matched closure clears; independent descendant closures survive and original launch authority remains revoked. Preserved input and settled Jobs are not automatically redispatched. Idempotency replay returns its historical response without clearing a later scope.

Local validation passes 1017 Rust workspace test executions (three ignored fixture entries, including subprocess workers exercised separately), 27 final focused authority/closure/stop/reopening cases, 49 HTTP cases and the production managed-worktree reopening case. All 52 SDK tests/type checking, generated-contract checks, workspace all-target Clippy, formatting and all 57 strict specifications pass; cross-spec lint reports zero errors and 21 warnings. Controlled cases cover Unknown/superseded/stale reviews, nested/new scopes, delayed native verification with changed Session and operation evidence, disposal/refusal, orphaned sweeps, projection corruption, history gaps, persisted old writer authority after restart and settled Job non-redispatch. Windows CI includes the new controlled suite; native acceptance is pending. Reviewed unknown native/orphan recovery, complete cross-process actor cancellation, exec/TUI and budget-triggered adoption remain required. No P1 milestone is accepted; P0 artifacts are unchanged.

## Retained native settlement for reopening proof

Checkout settlement can now acknowledge its durable activity record while retaining the original native lock. The Location host contract exposes an explicit retained form; its default refuses unsupported hosts rather than accepting consuming settlement. The production worktree host retains all source/target checkout leases until proof disposal. Unmanaged Locations use an empty proof. A failed acknowledgement returns no proof and retains recovery evidence.

An additional production-host case verifies that checkout removal refuses the retained lease and succeeds only after proof disposal. Native Windows/Linux acceptance is pending. This supplies the native ownership prerequisite; the matched scope/receipt and actor-evidence writer transaction, reviewed unknown-effect recovery and full client adoption remain required. Admission reopening is not enabled. No P1 milestone is accepted; P0 artifacts are unchanged.

POST `/api/v1/sessions/{sessionID}/stop-subtree` and generated `session.stopSubtree` now expose the owned local runtime sweep. ## Durable child result ownership and original target authority

Child result ownership now persists before native callbacks or result collection and is exclusive across runtimes sharing the database. The writer verifies the original source authority and independently refuses closed target ancestry. Existing-child claims retain target creation identity, parent links, ordinary interruption boundaries and subtree generations. Agent preparation and reviewed setup retry run under the held authority; resume/rebind/recovery checks the original owner rather than capturing a fresh target boundary. Guarded authority also retains durable operation revocation through inherited callbacks and writer transactions.

Owner release binds the original parent/child, remains writable after closure and revokes retained callback capability. A process that disappears without releasing leaves the held receipt. Subtree stop audits durable owners by source parent or target, including another runtime and reserved children not yet created. Result release is separate from native acknowledgement; Location, operation, tool and preparation evidence still governs unknown native effects.

A subprocess with its own writer verifies database exclusivity and acknowledged release. Child-sourced continuation remains valid through ordinary parent interruption and refuses an explicit subtree fence; ancestor callback capture retains the original child boundary. Native setup captures before Location admission and verifies before each new command and after sandbox preparation; the blocked second-command journal records NotDispatched. The runtime now tracks idle native/maintenance operations before Location admission with durable reservation, launch and settlement receipts. ## Durable subtree admission closure

The existing generation-only fence continues to permit fresh launch authority. An owned sweep can now additionally close admission durably with a scope operation ID. Closure survives restart and later generation-only fences. Capture and preflight read ancestry and closure in one SQL snapshot; the writer checks closed source and target ancestry even when an event has no captured bindings. An open parent cannot register a Job targeting a closed child. Root and descendant input, attempt resets, held release/promotion, child/Job registration, Step/tool dispatch, compaction and new permission/question requests refuse closed ancestry.

Ordinary input checks closure before staged rewind effects. Drain registration checks closure while holding its actor registry; Drain passes and idle native operations recheck before recovery/inference and before/after Location admission. A shell waiting for a Location cannot dispatch after closure. Terminal Job/tool/cancellation evidence remains writable. A shell already running records its internal output receipt and paired promotion after closure; ordinary prompt admission cannot set the receipt marker or wake inference through it.

## Tracked direct subtask ownership

Direct user subtask callbacks now use the durable delegation ledger and tracked owner. The external cancellation token is bound before dispatch. Disposing a caller signals cancellation while the native host stays tracked until it acknowledges completion; shutdown joins that owner. A reply/acceptance handshake retains callback ownership of a registered Job until the caller accepts delivery. Closed or cancelled delivery stops only the verified parent/child Job and awaits settlement. After accepted return, later caller cancellation leaves the independent Job running.

Hosts with launch-marker support receive the internal request ID and retain Reserved pre-effect admission. Legacy hosts without that contract start conservatively in Launching; uncertain host failures remain Unknown. Rejected Job delivery without a live cancellation owner records Unknown with the verified Job identity. A cancelled caller receives an acknowledgement error rather than a still-running Job as successful cancellation. Source-labelled controls bind lookup, launch markers and stop requests to the owning Session.

The disposal regression fails on unchanged production main a95cd01 because the host future disappears before observing cancellation. ## Callback and handoff admission fences

Explicit user-subtask/delegation callbacks and owned child preparation now retain captured source/ancestor authority through host awaits, with caller and worker lifetime cancellation. Nested callback work retains the original source boundary until handoff; independent spawned child Drains keep ordinary background semantics. Scoped futures are boxed so the actual native preparation paths pass at normal stack limits. Prepared public prompts, wake and held release retain authority through the reset/input writer transaction; native recreation checks inherited authority before each creation retry.

Writer checks now cover durable reservation/launch markers, scoped input, resumed attempts and background Job registration. Durable callback bindings also carry their input-bound operation IDs: the latest record must still be Pending and belong to the bound Session, so request cancellation refuses creation even after preflight while a Location lease waits. Existing Reserved launch markers require a live, unstopped local owner; missing/restarted/panicked ownership cannot advance them. Terminal cancellation/Job receipts remain writable after fencing. A Job registered before ordinary parent interruption remains independent even when its callback reply is delayed. Admission checks authority before committing a staged conversation revert.

The delayed callback, missing-owner marker and durable Location-claim regressions fail on unchanged production main 2990632. Local coverage additionally verifies caller cancellation, nested authority, source/ancestor boundaries, atomic input/resume/Job rollback, staged-revert preservation, late child preparation, ownership release and registered Job independence. Cross-spec lint has zero errors and 21 warnings. Workspace all-target Clippy passes; native CI acceptance remains pending.

## Captured child launch authority

Child dispatch now captures runtime-owned source/ancestor authority before approval and FIFO waits, carries it into fresh child creation and verifies it before each native checkout creation retry. Source interruption records a durable boundary for root Sessions as well as children. The explicit subtree fence primitive invalidates captured descendant launch tickets; ordinary ancestor Drain interruption preserves independent background-child authority. One SQL read snapshot binds parent identities, original creation event IDs, source interruption and ancestor subtree boundaries. Missing/cyclic ancestry, foreign runtime/source authority and deleted/recreated identities refuse admission.

Fresh child creation persists these bindings, and the single writer rechecks them before projecting a Session. Cancellation or ancestry changes between preflight and creation commit roll back both child history and projection and return a typed conflict. The real queued-launch regression fails on unchanged main a8b5d91: the queued call creates a second child after parent interruption. The fix passes locally. ## Owned child preparation cancellation

Public continuation, explicit wake, held release and automatic requested-input handoff now register child preparation before native work. The owned task retains exclusive execution ownership through host completion and admission; disposing the caller cancels preparation while the task remains tracked. Interruption records a durable pause boundary, cancels the registered token and waits for acknowledgement. Registration and the reset/admission transaction check that boundary, so late preparation success cannot admit a prompt or release held input. Shutdown cancels and joins preparation through existing runtime ownership without holding an outer lifecycle read across nested setup admission.

Host cancellation has a two-second acknowledgement bound. A timeout or panic records durable preparation-phase unknown evidence, retains native recovery evidence and refuses prompt, wake and resume after restart. Execution ownership is released before completion is signalled. The native regression fails on unchanged production efdad19: after interrupt returns, releasing setup still admits new input. It now passes for interrupt, caller disposal and shutdown, with no late file effect or inference. Controlled hosts cover disposal ownership, late-success held release and stalled/panicked restart refusal.

## Managed public child continuation

The public prompt path now prepares an existing managed child through the tool-host port while holding its exclusive execution owner. A clean-removed checkout recreates only from acknowledged removal evidence, retaining child identity, branch and original base with a new checkout binding. Prepared continuations retain reporting/cleanup ownership through Drain settlement. Successful completed answers apply current cleanup policy and record file/diff evidence in `session.child.continuation_settled.1`; failed/interrupted executions keep their checkout. During cleanup, prompt, wake, resume and internal admission cannot race new input into removal. Admission checks the finalization phase while holding Session state so input already accepted is visible before cleanup eligibility is chosen, without holding the global Drain lock during database writes. Cleanup approval waits observe cancellation; interruption and shutdown keep files and acknowledge settlement before releasing ownership.

Unacknowledged settlement and host panics record durable unknown evidence; replay refuses normal prompt, resume, shell, compaction and context repair until recovery. The child-thread API reports failed settlement instead of completion. Known reporting failures stay visible and retain native activity evidence. Preparation releases outer lifecycle admission before invoking existing setup ownership, then reacquires admission before committing input, avoiding nested-read shutdown blockage.

## Owned long-root repository inspection

Windows long-root status, tracked summaries and ignored-file enumeration now use the existing runtime-owned execution port to launch `cyber-sandbox-exec --git-inspect` from verified short linked metadata. The helper opens that metadata with libgit2 and sets the real worktree only in memory. It checks canonical worktree/common-directory identity, the linked backpointer, the expected branch and stable HEAD. Before any libgit2 repository access, the single-threaded helper points system/global/XDG/program-data configuration searches at an owned empty directory. Invalid ambient HOME/XDG configuration is covered by the process regression. Fixed isolated overrides disable hooks/fsmonitor and enable long paths; configuration and index writes are excluded from the inspection engine. The runtime port retains sandbox policy, credential filtering, bounded output, process-tree ownership, cancellation and deadlines. Unsupported helper/port availability refuses execution.

This replaces the prior test-only candidate for Windows roots at least 240 UTF-16 units long. Other paths retain native Git. Existing ignored-inclusion fingerprint checks and bounded untracked-file statistics remain in the core manager. git2 now has transport features disabled as a production dependency. The original mandatory native long-root lifecycle assertion remains unchanged; the fixture invokes the actual helper and an additional native protocol step checks malformed/oversized requests and identity refusal.

Local validation passes 902 workspace test executions (two opt-in measurements ignored), followed by 98 focused repository/helper/isolated-child tests after the final identity check and runtime regression. The added actual child-runtime case covers long-root file output, summaries, verified listing and same-identity resume using explicitly selected full access; it does not prove confinement. Workspace all-target Clippy, formatting, generated contracts and all 57 strict specs pass; cross-spec lint reports zero errors and 21 warnings. Coverage includes long-root summaries, ignored/untracked/tracked changes, staged/deleted library status, unchanged index/config/links and rejected foreign identities/overrides. Native Windows acceptance, runtime confinement and complete lifecycle coverage remain open. No P1 milestone or P0 local-model gate is accepted by this change.

## Latest CI follow-up

Completed native diagnostic CI for 553f718 still fails only the mandatory long-root Git status case ([Windows job](https://github.com/CyberdyneCorp/cybercode-cli/actions/runs/37713391787/job/113104254009)). The configuration-before-worktree controls show staged diff succeeding, while unstaged diff and untracked enumeration both refuse the worktree. Launching through the existing short alias still fails current-directory resolution with a filename-too-long error; disabling symlink normalization still refuses worktree setup. These controls do not supply a complete status implementation, so composing the three commands cannot close the gate. The mandatory assertion remains unchanged and no production fallback is accepted. Subsequent native steps complete successfully; full Windows confinement and supported long-root status remain open.


The long-root failure investigation now compares read-only unstaged/staged diff and untracked-file enumeration at the same explicit metadata/verified short-alias boundary as status. These labelled controls run only after the mandatory native status assertion has already failed; they never turn that failure into acceptance. External diff drivers and text conversion are disabled. The hypothesis is configuration initialization ordering: Git's [builtin dispatch](https://github.com/git-for-windows/git/blob/v2.55.0.windows.5/git.c) performs status worktree setup before command-specific core configuration, while [diff](https://github.com/git-for-windows/git/blob/v2.55.0.windows.5/builtin/diff.c) and [ls-files](https://github.com/git-for-windows/git/blob/v2.55.0.windows.5/builtin/ls-files.c) defer their worktree setup. Its [Windows path implementation](https://github.com/git-for-windows/git/blob/v2.55.0.windows.5/compat/mingw.c) expands aliases during chdir and uses fixed MAX_PATH buffers for getcwd. The native controls must prove or refute this hypothesis before any production status replacement is accepted. Local repository coverage passes 50 tests (one opt-in measurement ignored); this does not execute the Windows-only controls or close the long-path gate.

Completed CI for d519b5b passes every non-Windows job, including x64 macOS and all six platform builds. Windows fails only the mandatory long-root status assertion ([Windows job](https://github.com/CyberdyneCorp/cybercode-cli/actions/runs/37657621416/job/112916648810)); the corrected child-context assertion and new Job accounting regressions pass natively. This supersedes the pending acceptance for those focused corrections below, without accepting complete Windows enforcement, subtree cancellation or a full P1 milestone.

Completed CI for d31821e fails Windows long-root Git status and the isolated-child context assertion ([Windows job](https://github.com/CyberdyneCorp/cybercode-cli/actions/runs/37650767280/job/112893185933)). The x64 macOS build fails while downloading the stable Rust channel metadata from static.rust-lang.org, before project compilation ([macOS build job](https://github.com/CyberdyneCorp/cybercode-cli/actions/runs/37650767280/job/112893185982)). These are distinct failures, not one Windows blocker.

## Background Job subtree accounting follow-up

Job settlement now reads own and descendant counters together from SQL, preserving durable nested/hidden charges even when runtime Session caches have not observed external billing writes. Fresh attempts record a complete zero baseline; resumed children capture the durable subtree baseline so earlier spending is not charged again. Deleted descendants retain billed charges. Unpriced nested calls, incomplete historical attribution, decreasing counters and legacy own-only baselines disclose uncertainty; legacy baselines retain only the known own lower bound rather than guessing prior descendant spending.

Two durable-billing regressions fail on unchanged main 75d7a6b. All 65 focused Job/resume/isolated-child tests and workspace all-target Clippy pass after the fix, including descendant deletion, nested unpriced usage, resumed attempt deltas/restart persistence and legacy baseline uncertainty. Strict validation passes all 57 specs; generated contracts and formatting pass, and cross-spec lint reports zero errors and 21 warnings. Existing native Job/resume CI includes these regressions; native acceptance remains pending. Spending after Job settlement from independently running descendants, complete subtree cancellation with admission-generation fences, reservations, daily caps and the full P1 goal remain open. P0 artifacts are unchanged.

## Durable descendant token-class accounting increment

Ancestor billing now retains separate input, output, reasoning, cache-read and cache-write counters alongside existing combined tokens and cost. These counters and receipt evidence are written atomically and survive descendant deletion. Session detail/list responses and the generated SDK expose the class counters plus an independent completeness flag. Older client snapshots default to unknown class attribution rather than claiming a complete zero.

The migration reconstructs classes from surviving source events without changing retained financial receipts. Missing/purged or sparse historical class evidence remains explicitly incomplete; new charges cannot erase that uncertainty. Fresh Sessions begin with complete zero counters. Malformed stored class projections fail the read rather than becoming zero. Sparse new reports retain known class amounts but cannot claim complete attribution.

Two class-retention assertions fail on unchanged main 80049f3. Cross-spec lint reports zero errors and 21 warnings. Native acceptance is pending. `GET /api/v1/usage?scope=session&id=…` now returns own, descendant and combined usage from one SQL snapshot, independently of cached conversation state. ## Atomic TUI cost refresh increment

`/cost` and R now request the scope-addressed usage API directly, without fetching conversation history or combining cached own amounts with separately read descendants. The cost view has independent snapshot ownership. This supersedes the earlier pending atomic view wiring. Report parsing requires the expected Session/scope, every token class and completeness field, and internally consistent totals. Failed/malformed refreshes preserve the last observed snapshot with an explicit failure message. Request generations prevent late responses from overwriting a newer refresh; switching Sessions clears the view while preserving generation ordering across a return to the original Session. Ordinary conversation refreshes cannot replace the atomic cost snapshot.

The actual HTTP route regression fails on unchanged main 61c74d5 before the change. ## Delivery sequence

### Ordinary exec subtree accounting increment

Ordinary exec now captures own/descendant billing before prompt submission, polls every 200 ms and performs a bounded final snapshot read. Final JSON includes spending since that baseline, all five own token classes, separate own/descendant totals and attribution completeness. Reused Session history is excluded; hidden and descendant calls are observed without needing a parent step event. Durable snapshots replace previous totals, and later parent step events cannot duplicate already observed charges. Counter regression, malformed attribution or a foreign Session snapshot refuses accounting while preserving previously known totals. Budgeted runs refuse missing/incomplete descendant attribution before sending the prompt; unbudgeted legacy servers retain the known own lower bound and explicitly disclose unknown descendants.

Three regressions fail on unchanged production main 553f718 with the corrected blocking HTTP fixture. All 59 CLI test executions pass locally; the final five binary-level accounting tests and workspace all-target Clippy pass again after fixture extraction. Those cases cover reused-history exclusion, descendant-only budget exhaustion, pre-prompt legacy refusal, unbudgeted legacy uncertainty and a server that never acknowledges interruption. The last case is bounded and reports uncertainty instead of waiting indefinitely. Formatting, generated contracts and all 57 strict specs pass; cross-spec lint remains zero errors and 21 warnings. Native CI now includes ordinary accounting tests; new native acceptance is pending.

Follow-up validation requires every own token counter plus step/unpriced counters to be present integers; missing/null counters can no longer become zero. A strengthened snapshot regression fails on unchanged main c67fd7d before that correction. All 59 CLI tests and workspace all-target Clippy pass again after the strict-counter fix.

This is a soft observation window over the selected Session subtree, including other activity recorded there during execution. It does not prove exclusive per-prompt ownership, conservative reservations, ancestor warning presentation, daily caps or descendant task cancellation. Those contracts, durable admission-generation fences, Windows confinement, the remaining long-root investigation and the full P1 goal stay open. P0 artifacts are unchanged. The completed native diagnostic job for 553f718 still fails the mandatory repository status case; the inspected controls are recorded in Latest CI follow-up above.
